## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear route of Capital, Deployment and Change (slides 2, 3, 28, 49), and most chart slides pair a plain headline with a subtitle that states the point, such as slide 8 with its comparison to telecoms and oil and gas. The visual system is disciplined: one serif and one sans, a red and charcoal palette, a consistent footer and source line, and charts that use red to isolate the series that matters. Weaknesses are small text in chart legends and footers for a conference room, a repeated circle-and-arrow diagram that adds little beyond its words (36, 42, 44, 59, 67), and several near-empty quote and divider slides (50, 51, 60) that slow the rhythm across 79 pages.

**Worth keeping:** The headline-plus-subtitle chart format with red highlighting on slide 8, the section dividers, and the consistent footer and source lines.

**Most useful repair:** Enlarge legends, axis labels and footer text on the chart slides, and add direct labels so the charts read from the back of a room.

**What would change the score:** Cut or merge the sparse quote slides (50, 51, 60) and vary the circle diagrams so that slides 36, 42 and 44 carry more than short phrases in circles.

_Confidence medium. 72 of 79 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and a three-part frame of Capital, Deployment and Change, then moves into the capital section, which tracks platform shifts and the rising capital spending on AI infrastructure by large technology companies, including chips, data centres and balance-sheet strain. The deployment section looks at user adoption, workplace use, how companies move from pilots to daily use, and the software market. The change section asks what automation means for jobs and industries, and what new questions AI makes possible. The closing slides return to the theme that the outcome is uncertain and that the useful work is asking the right questions. Charts carry most of the argument, with short headline titles and subtitles that state the point of each chart. Quotes from executives appear as separate slides between sections to break up the data. The author's name and date appear in a consistent footer. Slides worth studying include the capex chart (slide 8), the quote slides (7 and 17), and the framework slides on tasks and jobs (67 and 68).

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Benedict Evans AI eats the world deck have, and how is it structured?**

The deck has 79 slides. Slide 2 frames it in three parts, Capital, Deployment and Change, after the title slide.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about AI infrastructure capex by big technology companies?**

Slide 8, titled 'A capex explosion', says $700bn is planned in 2026 for the big four. For comparison, it lists global Telecoms at about $300bn and Oil & Gas at about $1tr.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about ChatGPT weekly users?**

Slide 29, titled 'Everyone is already using this!', says OpenAI reports 900m+ weekly users but only 5% are paying.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about Nvidia's revenue and capacity?**

Slide 9, titled 'Nvidia can't keep up', charts quarterly revenue in $bn for Nvidia and Intel from 2000 to 2025. Its subtitle says Nvidia can't get TSMC to increase capacity fast enough.

Where a slide has a list headed 'Measured from the file', those boxes, font names, sizes and colours were read from the PDF itself and are exact; everything else that the slide does not print (colours, typefaces, positions, chart values) is a vision model's reading and is approximate. **Layout**

A single full-width chart slide on a three-band structure: a grey header band holds the title and subtitle, a white central panel holds the chart, and a grey footer band holds the source line on the left and the author, date and page number on the right. Text left edge is aligned at about 20.5% of slide width, with the title, subtitle, chart heading, y-axis labels and source line all sharing that left edge; the chart plot runs to about 96% of width. The title sits top-left, the page number sits bottom-right at about 95% of width.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Type: line chart with a single smoothed red series. Title: 'Microsoft OS share of global computer unit sales'. Y axis: percentage share of global computer unit sales, 0% to 100%, gridlines and labels at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%. X axis: years, 1980 to about 2024, labelled at 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010 and 2020. Series values (none printed; all about): 1980 about 0%; 1982 about 9%; 1983 about 26%; 1985 about 49%; 1988 about 80%; 1990 about 84%; 1991 about 77% (local minimum); 1995 about 91%; 1998 about 97% (peak); 2000 about 97%; 2004 about 97%; 2008 about 91%; 2010 about 70%; 2011 about 48%; 2013 about 20%; 2016 about 11% (low); 2018 about 12%; 2021 about 16% (local high); 2024 about 12%. Legend: none. Highlighted points: none, apart from the 1998 peak and the 2016 trough being the visual extremes. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey lines at each 25% step spanning the plot width. The x axis is a solid thin grey baseline at 0%. No markers on the line.

**Design**

Background is light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF middle panel. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.6% of height. Chart heading in MessinaSans-SemiBold sans-serif, #545554, about 2.2%. Axis labels in MessinaSans-Regular sans-serif, #545554, about 1.85%. Source line in MessinaSans-Regular, #545454, about 1.85%. Author name in MessinaSans-Bold, #A6A6A6; date and dash in MessinaSans-Book, #A6A6A6; page number in MessinaSans-Book, #A6A6A6; all about 1.85%. Accent colour is the red chart line, about #E8473F. Line style: series stroke about 3 px at slide scale, round joins, no fill. Gridlines dotted grey about #BFBFBF, 1 px. Baseline solid grey about #8C8C8C. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A stacked bar chart with a clear subtitle that states the takeaway, with the source and footnote placed below the chart.
- Review: The deck has a clear route of Capital, Deployment and Change (slides 2, 3, 28, 49), and most chart slides pair a plain headline with a subtitle that states the point, such as slide 8 with its comparison to telecoms and oil and gas.
- Worth keeping: The headline-plus-subtitle chart format with red highlighting on slide 8, the section dividers, and the consistent footer and source lines.

**Text**

A capex explosion / $700bn planned in 2026 for the big four (for comparison, global Telecoms is ~$300bn and Oil & Gas is ~$1tr) / Annual capex, 2010-2026e ($bn) / Meta / AWS* / Alphabet / Microsoft / 800 / 700 / 600 / 500 / 400 / 300 / 200 / 100 / 0 / 2010 / 2011 / 2012 / 2013 / 2014 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / 2022 / 2023 / 2024 / 2025 / 2026 / guidance / Source: Companies, company guidance. Includes capital leases / * Amazon does not break out AWS capex directly but reports it as the vast majority / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 8

**Layout**

Two-band slide: a light grey top band (y 0 to 20.5%) holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at x 20.5% with the title at y 5.6%. A white content band (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holds the chart, with the chart title at y 27.2% left-aligned at x 20.6%, the legend in the left margin at x 4.8%, and the plot area spanning x about 17.8% to 95%. A grey footer band (y 86.1% to 100%) holds the source lines left-aligned at x 20.5%, the author and date at x 74.9% to 89%, and the page number '8' at x 95%.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked column chart titled 'Annual capex, 2010-2026e ($bn)'. Y axis: '$bn' implied by chart title, range 0 to 800, labelled every 100, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each 100 step. X axis: years 2010 to 2026, with 2026 labelled 'guidance' beneath; 2026 is a forecast. Series stacked bottom to top: Microsoft (red), Alphabet (salmon), AWS* (pale pink), Meta (dark grey). Printed values: none; estimates marked 'about'. Estimated column totals: 2010 about 5; 2011 about 0; 2012 about 0; 2013 about 12; 2014 about 16; 2015 about 22; 2016 about 36; 2017 about 35; 2018 about 62; 2019 about 63; 2020 about 60; 2021 about 93; 2022 about 120; 2023 about 131; 2024 about 225; 2025 about 380; 2026 guidance about 690. Estimated 2025 segments: Microsoft about 118, Alphabet about 92, AWS about 100, Meta about 70. Estimated 2026 guidance segments: Microsoft about 190, Alphabet about 182, AWS about 180, Meta about 137. Legend: top to bottom Meta (dark grey square), AWS* (pale pink square), Alphabet (salmon square), Microsoft (red square). No highlighted points; bars are separated by thin white gaps. Bars are solid fills with no gradients.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 top and bottom bands with a white #FFFFFF central band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Flat, minimal editorial style. Background of the top and footer bands is light grey #F1F1F1 and the content band is white #FFFFFF. Title and subtitle are set in a serif (MessinaModern-Regular) in grey #545454. Chart labels, legend and source use a humanist sans (MessinaSans-Regular) in grey #545454. Chart title is MessinaSans-SemiBold #545454. Author name is MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6 and date is MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Title size about 5.56% of slide height, subtitle about 2.59%, body and labels about 1.85%. Accent palette: Microsoft red #EE4B3B, Alphabet salmon #F4A29C, AWS pale pink #F9DAD7, Meta dark grey #505050. Gridlines are thin dotted grey. No shadows, no corner radii, no gradients.

### Slide 9

**Text**

Nvidia can’t keep up / (And can’t get TSMC to increase capacity fast enough) / Quarterly revenue ($bn) / Intel / Nvidia / 70 / 60 / 50 / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / 2025 / Source: Companies / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 9

**Layout**

A full-width light grey header band holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about 20.5% of slide width, which is the same left edge as the chart title, the x-axis start and the source line. A white content band runs between the header and a light grey footer. The chart fills the middle, with its legend in the left margin at about 4.8% and the plot area spanning about 20.5% to 95.3% of width. The footer holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number at about 95% of width near the right margin.

**Visuals**

One line chart titled 'Quarterly revenue ($bn)'. Y-axis: quarterly revenue in $bn, labelled 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each 10 step and a solid thin grey baseline at 0. X-axis: years 2000 to 2025 labelled at every fifth year, plotted quarterly. Series 1, Intel, dark grey line (about #545454), about 8 at 2000, about 9 around 2001, about 7 around 2002, about 10 around 2007, about 12 at 2009 low, about 14 around 2011, about 15 around 2014, about 20 around 2019 to 2020 and a peak of about 20.5 around 2022, falling to about 12 around 2023, then about 14 at 2025. Series 2, Nvidia, red line (about #E8473F), about 0.5 from 2000 to 2016, about 2 around 2017, about 3 around 2018, about 6 around 2020 to 2021, about 8 around 2022, a dip to about 6 at 2023, then a steep rise to about 17 at 2024, about 35 at mid-2024, about 47 at late 2025 and about 68 at the last plotted point. No values are printed on the chart, so all values are estimates marked 'about'. Legend at upper left of the plot, outside it, with a short dark grey line for Intel and a short red line for Nvidia. No points are highlighted.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer areas, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart area (the white band covers y 20.5% to 86.1%). Light grey header and footer bands (#F1F1F1) framing a white content band. Title in a modern serif (Messina Modern Regular, close to a transitional serif), dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, regular weight. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels in a grotesque sans-serif (Messina Sans), regular and semibold weights, #545454 to #535353, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. Accent colour is the red Nvidia line (#E8473F) against the dark grey Intel line. Footer text is Messina Sans, Bold for the author name and Book for the date and page number, in light grey #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height. Lines are thin (about 2 px at this size), with dotted grey gridlines, no shadows, no rounded corners.

### Slide 10

**Text**

A new semiconductor investment cycle? / Unprecedented surge in chip demand (but semis have always been a cyclical industry) / Monthly global semiconductor billings ($bn, 2026 dollars) / 125 / 100 / 75 / 50 / 25 / 0 / Jan 1990 / Jan 2000 / Jan 2010 / Jan 2020 / Mar 2026 / Source: WSTS, US Census / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 10

**Layout**

Three horizontal bands: a light grey #F1F1F1 header band (top about 20% of the slide) holding a left-aligned serif title and subtitle, a white middle band (about 20% to 86%) holding a single full-width line chart, and a light grey #F1F1F1 footer strip (below about 86%) holding the source line at left and author, date and page number at right. Text is left-aligned to a common left margin at about x 20.5%; the title sits near the top at y 5.6%, the page number sits at far right near x 94.5%, y 91%, and the chart spans from about x 18% to 97%.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Type: continuous time-series line chart, single series, no markers. Chart title: 'Monthly global semiconductor billings ($bn, 2026 dollars)'. Y-axis: 0 to 125 $bn, labelled every 25 (0, 25, 50, 75, 100, 125), light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X-axis: monthly from Jan 1990 to Mar 2026, labelled Jan 1990, Jan 2000, Jan 2010, Jan 2020, Mar 2026; solid thin grey baseline at 0. Series: red line about #EE4035, roughly 2.5 px wide, rising from about 10 in 1990 (about 10 to 12 through the early 1990s), about 25 to 30 in 1996 to 1997, a peak about 40 in late 1999, a dip to about 17 in 2001 to 2002, about 30 to 40 in 2005 to 2008, a dip to about 20 in late 2008, about 40 to 50 in 2012 to 2016, about 50 to 60 in 2018 to 2019, a dip to about 40 in 2019 to 2020, about 55 to 62 in 2021, a dip to about 41 in 2023, a sharp climb to about 80 to 85 in 2024 to 2025, and ending at about 116 at Mar 2026 at the right edge of the plot. No legend. Highlighted point: the final point at Mar 2026, about 116, where the line runs off the top-right of the plot area steeply upward.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about y 20.5% to 86.1%. Flat, minimal palette. Header and footer bands and the page background are light grey #F1F1F1; the chart band is white #FFFFFF. Title text in a serif (Messina Modern Regular, a modern serif, regular weight) in #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, set in sentence case. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title in a sans-serif (Messina Sans SemiBold) in #545554, about 2.2% of height. Axis labels and source line in a sans-serif (Messina Sans Regular) in #545554 / #545454, about 1.85% of height. Footer author name in Messina Sans Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in Messina Sans Book #A6A6A6, all about 1.85% of height. Accent colour is the red series line #EE4035. Lines: gridlines are thin dotted mid-grey; baseline is a thin solid grey rule. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a white horizontal band across the middle; the title and subtitle sit top-left in a grey header strip, aligned to a shared left edge at about 20.5% of slide width. The chart occupies the white band from about 20% to 96% of width, with a legend at far left (about 4.8% x) and a source line and footer (author, date, slide number) in the bottom grey strip, the slide number at the right margin near 95%.

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart titled 'Capex for leading semiconductor companies ($bn)'. Y-axis: $bn, 0 to 150, with labelled gridlines at 0, 50, 100 and 150 (dotted light grey lines; solid grey baseline at 0). X-axis: calendar years 2010 to 2025 and '2026 guidance' (the final label wraps to two lines). Three stacked series, bottom to top: TSMC (red), Intel (dark grey), Samsung Electronics (light grey); thin white separators between segments. Legend at top-left of chart: Samsung Electronics (light grey swatch), Intel (dark grey swatch), TSMC (red swatch), listed top to bottom. No values are printed on the bars; values below are estimates marked 'about', read against the axis (TSMC / Intel / Samsung segments, total in brackets): 2010 about 6 / 5 / 14 (about 25); 2011 about 7 / 11 / 14 (about 32); 2012 about 8 / 11 / 15 (about 34); 2013 about 9 / 11 / 15 (about 35); 2014 about 9 / 10 / 15 (about 34); 2015 about 8 / 7 / 18 (about 33); 2016 about 10 / 10 / 16 (about 36); 2017 about 10 / 13 / 28 (about 51); 2018 about 10 / 15 / 20 (about 45); 2019 about 15 / 16 / 17 (about 48); 2020 about 16 / 14 / 26 (about 56); 2021 about 27 / 20 / 32 (about 79); 2022 about 35 / 24 / 34 (about 93); 2023 about 30 / 26 / 39 (about 95); 2024 about 30 / 24 / 35 (about 89); 2025 about 41 / 14 / 33 (about 88); 2026 guidance about 56 / 15 / 74 (about 145). No highlighted points; the 2026 guidance bar is the tallest and is not visually distinguished beyond its label.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the full slide, with a white (#FFFFFF) band from 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Light grey #F1F1F1 page with a white central band; titles in dark grey #545454 serif, body and chart labels #545454 / #535353 sans-serif, accents in TSMC red #E94B3C, Intel dark grey #535353 and Samsung light grey #A7A7A7; footer text #A6A6A6. Title is a regular-weight high-contrast serif (Messina Modern Regular) at about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle serif regular at about 2.6%; chart title is semibold sans (Messina Sans SemiBold) at about 2.2%; axis, legend and footer text is regular sans (Messina Sans) at about 1.9%; the author name is bold and the date and page number are book weight. Lines are thin dotted light grey gridlines and a solid grey baseline; no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 12

**Text**

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5% of height) holds the title and subtitle, both left-aligned to a shared left edge at about 20.5% of slide width. A white content band runs from 20.5% to 86.1% of height and holds a legend at the far left (x 4.8%) and a single full-width line chart whose plot area spans roughly x 18% to 95.5%. A light grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number at x 94.5%, right margin about 4.5%. No separate slide-number box beyond the footer page number.

**Visuals**

Chart: line chart, two series. Title printed above the plot: 'US construction value ($bn, 2026 dollars, seasonally adjusted annual rate)'. Y axis: 'US construction value' in $bn, 2026 dollars, seasonally adjusted annual rate, range 0 to 125, gridlines and labels at 0, 25, 50, 75, 100, 125; no axis title printed beyond the chart title. X axis: time, Jan 1995 to about Jan 2025 (last tick at Jan 2025 with series running slightly past it), labelled ticks at Jan 1995, Jan 2000, Jan 2005, Jan 2010, Jan 2015, Jan 2020, Jan 2025. Series 1 'Offices', dark grey line (#545454), no printed values; about 53 in Jan 1995, rising to a peak of about 115 around Jan 2001, falling to about 87 by Jan 2003, a trough of about 55 in 2003, a second peak of about 95 in 2007, a fall to a low of about 35 in 2011, a rise to about 85 in 2015, a peak of about 95 in 2019/2020, falling to about 46 at the end in 2025. Series 2 'Data centres*', red line (#E8473B), starts about 2 in 2014, about 4 in 2015, about 10 in 2018, about 12 in 2020, about 20 in 2023, about 35 in 2024, ending about 49 in 2025, where it crosses above the Offices line. Legend at upper left of the plot: dark grey short line with 'Offices', red short line with 'Data centres*'. No highlighted points or annotations. Gridlines: horizontal dotted light grey lines at 25, 50, 75, 100, 125; solid mid grey baseline at 0.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide fill, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of height. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands with a white (#FFFFFF) content band between them. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, not bold. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.6% of height. Author name in MessinaSans-Bold, light grey #A6A6A6; date and page number in MessinaSans-Book, #A6A6A6. Source line in MessinaSans-Regular, #545454, about 1.8% of height. Data centres series in red #E8473B; Offices series in dark grey #545454. Gridlines dotted light grey (about #BFBFBF, thin 0.5pt). Baseline solid grey about #8C8C8C, 1pt. Lines are smooth-free, 2pt weight, with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Visuals**

Chart: clustered column chart with a bar for every year, grouped by company, titled 'Annual capex/sales, CY 2010 to 2026e'. ### Slide 14

Here comes the ‘structure’
As spending surges, companies scramble to manage the balance sheet
CNBC window: CNBC / LIVESTREAM / SIG / TECH / Meta partners with Blue Owl Capital or / $27 billion AI data center project / PUBLISHED TUE, OCT 21 2025•5:36 PM EDT / Jonathan Vanian / @/IN/JONATHAN-VANIAN-B704432/ / WATCH LIVE / KEY POINTS / Meta said Tuesday that it formed a joint venture agreement with Blue Owl Capital in a deal worth $27 billion to fund and develop the massive Hyperion data center in rural Louisiana. / The asset management firm will own 80% in the joint venture, while Meta will retain a 20% stake and oversee the construction and property management services of the data center. / Meta has been spending heavily on AI amid a broader race with other tech giants like Alphabet and ChatGPT-maker OpenAI, which are also developing gigantic data centers to power future AI models.
Bloomberg window: Bloomberg / Oracle: Shares Tumble | Oracle's $300 Billion AI Bet | AI-Fueled Rent Spree | L / Markets / Oracle Debt Trades Like Junk as Bond, CDS Spreads Flare / By Caleb Mutua / December 12, 2025 at 3:03 PM EST / Updated on December 12, 2025 at 4:09 PM EST / Save / Translate / 3:05 / Takeaways by Bloomberg AI / Oracle Corp.'s new investment-grade notes are now trading more like junk bonds due to delays in data center completion dates and fears about profits from artificial intelligence investments. / The premium on Oracle's 5.2% notes maturing in 2035 has widened to 1.71 percentage points, and the yield on the notes has reached 5.9%, higher than the average for a basket of bonds in the highest tier of junk territory. / The cost of protecting Oracle's debt against default has risen, and some investors are hedging their exposure, with one investor saying that funding speculation in the tech market "isn't a recipe for success".

**Visuals**

Three browser-window screenshots of news websites, each with a macOS-style title bar of three traffic-light dots (red, yellow, green) at its top-left corner and a rounded-corner white window frame with a soft drop shadow. (2) Centre, Bloomberg article page: black header bar with hamburger icon, Bloomberg wordmark and user icon; a ticker row of headline links; article headline with byline and timestamps; Save, Translate and 3:05 audio buttons as outlined pill shapes; a bordered AI summary box titled Takeaways by Bloomberg AI with three bullets; sits in front of both other windows. (3) Right, Financial Times page: pale peach header with FINANCIAL TIMES masthead in black serif capitals, hamburger and user icons; below is a photograph in a black band: black-and-white photograph of a chain-link fence enclosing a construction site with a partly built structure and small trees on flat ground, with red diagonal lines drawn across it as an overlay and a white caption at bottom right; below that a pink-red Big Tech tag, a '+ Add to myFT' outlined pill, a large bold serif headline, a short black rule and a sans-serif standfirst. The FT window is partly hidden on its left edge by the Bloomberg window.

On the screenshot: EXPLORE OUR BRANDS / DJIA Futures 46429.00 0.10% / S&P 500 Futures 6711.00 0.13% / Nasdaq Futures / WSJ / TECHNOLOGY / OpenAI Unveils Plans for Seemingly Limitless Expansion of Computing Power / In Texas prairie, startup showcases ground zero of AI boom and its plans to shepherd $1 trillion in infrastructure spending / By Berber Jin Follow / Updated Sept. 23, 2025 at 7:13 pm ET

**Layout**

Two equal vertical columns: the left half is a light grey panel holding a left-aligned text block, and the right half is white holding a single browser-window screenshot. The title sits at upper-middle of the left column, with three paragraphs stacked beneath it, all left-aligned to a common left margin of about 4.6% of slide width. The source line sits bottom-left and the footer (author, date, slide number) sits bottom-right, on a shared baseline near 91% of slide height.

**Visuals**

One photograph-style screenshot of a Wall Street Journal article mocked up in a browser window, shown as a single image on the right half. The window has a white rounded frame with three traffic-light dots at top left (red, yellow, green), a black bar reading EXPLORE OUR BRANDS, a stock ticker strip (DJIA Futures 46429.00 0.10% up arrow; S&P 500 Futures 6711.00 0.13% up arrow; Nasdaq Futures cut off at the edge), a hamburger menu icon and the WSJ masthead at centre, then the article: a blue TECHNOLOGY kicker, a black serif headline, a grey deck, byline and a Follow button, an italic update timestamp, and a row of outline icons (share, bookmark, text size AA, comment, headphones). The lower part of the window shows a photograph cropped at the bottom edge: five men seated or standing at a panel in front of a dark backdrop with ORACLE lettering in white neon-style signage; the man third from left holds a black microphone; the men wear blue, grey and white shirts, and the two at the right wear dark suits. Lighting is soft and even with a dark, slightly purple-tinted backdrop; the photo is full-width within the window and cropped at the bottom. The window has a soft drop shadow.

**Design**

Background: Left half light grey #F2F2F2; right half white #FFFFFF. Two-tone split layout with a light grey left panel and white right panel, no gradients. Title and body text are dark grey #545454 in a Messina Modern Regular serif-style face, with the title at about 5.6% of slide height and body paragraphs at about 3.3%. The source line is dark grey #545454 in Messina Sans Book, about 1.9%. The footer is light grey #A6A6A6, with the author name in Messina Sans Bold and the date in Messina Sans Book, both about 1.9%. The browser window uses a white rounded rectangle with a soft shadow and small corner radius, traffic-light dots in red, yellow and green, and a near-black #1A1A1A bar for the WSJ strip. No accent colour beyond the screenshot's blue kicker.

**Layout**

Full-bleed slide with a light grey header band (top 0 to about 20.5% of height) holding the left-aligned serif title and subtitle, a white chart band from about 20.5% to 86% of height, and a light grey footer band below it. Content sits on a left margin of about 20.5% of width; the legend sits outside that margin at about 4.8% from the left. Footer source lines are left-aligned at 20.5%, and author, date and page number are on the right at about 74.9% to 95.5% width, page number at the far right.

**Visuals**

Line chart with two series, titled 'Reported monthly revenue ($bn)*'. Y axis: labelled 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 in $bn, gridlines dotted grey at each integer, solid grey baseline at 0, range 0 to about 4.4. X axis: labelled Dec 2022, Jun 2023, Dec 2023, Jun 2024, Dec 2024, Jun 2025, Dec 2025, Jun 2026 (semi-annual ticks). Series 1, 'OpenAI (net)', red #E8453C line with round markers: about 0 at Dec 2022, about 0.1 at early 2023, about 0.16 mid 2023, about 0.27 at late 2023, about 0.46 early 2024, about 0.55 mid 2024, about 0.78 at early 2025, about 0.91 at mid 2025, about 1.53 at late 2025, about 1.91 at early 2026 (last point). Series 2, 'Anthropic (gross)', dark grey #4D4D4D line with round markers: about 0 at Dec 2023, about 0.07 at Dec 2024, about 0.38 at mid 2025, about 0.54 at late 2025, about 0.69 at late 2025, about 1.07 at early 2026, about 1.45 at early 2026, about 2.29 at early 2026, about 3.49 at mid 2026 (last point). Values are estimates from the plotted positions as no values are printed. Legend at upper left of plot: red dot and 'OpenAI (net)', grey dot and 'Anthropic (gross)'. No highlighted points. Chart spans roughly x 19.6% to 93.7% and y 34.7% to 81.9%.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of height. Warm-neutral minimal corporate look. Header and footer bands #F1F1F1, chart band #FFFFFF. Title and subtitle in #545454 MessinaModern-Regular, a serif with regular weight. Chart title and labels in MessinaSans, sans-serif; chart title SemiBold #545554, legend and axis labels Regular #535353. Title size about 5.56% of slide height, subtitle 2.59%, body, axis and footer text 1.85% to 2.22%. Accent colours: red #E8453C for OpenAI and dark grey #4D4D4D for Anthropic. Footer author name Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number Book #A6A6A6. Lines are thin dotted grey gridlines (#BFBFBF) and a solid grey baseline; line series about 2px stroke with round markers about 1.2% of width. No shadows, gradients or rounded shapes.

### Slide 19

**Text**

“Wait, how much did we spend?!”
Applied AI
Uber CTO Shows How Claude Code Can Blow Up AI Budgets
Uber’s surging use of AI coding tools, particularly Anthropic’s Claude Code, has maxed out its full year AI budget just a few months into 2026, according to chief technology officer Praveen Neppalli Naga.
“I'm back to the drawing board because the budget I thought I would need is blown away already,” Neppalli Naga said in an interview.
Neppalli Naga’s comments highlight the challenges for companies trying to take advantage of new AI tools while also keeping costs under control. Uber is one of a number of companies that say they have encouraged employees to use AI as much as possible, creating internal “leader boards” ranking software engineers who use the most AI.
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
19

**Layout**

Two equal halves split vertically at 50%: the left half holds a large quotation vertically centred in the upper-middle area, left-aligned at about 4.6% from the left edge and starting at about 25.6% from the top. The right half holds a single browser-window screenshot centred horizontally on the right half, with the footer line (author, dash, date, and page number 19) on one baseline at about 91% from the top, right of centre, with no title bar or header; margins are about 4.6% left and about 5% right.

**Visuals**

One photograph-style screenshot of a web article shown inside a browser window mockup, boxed (not full-bleed), with a soft drop shadow. The window has rounded corners of about 2% of slide height and a light grey-white title strip containing three traffic-light dots: red #FF5F57, yellow #FEBC2E, green #28C840, at left. Below the title strip is a dark charcoal navigation bar #2E3338 containing, left to right: a three-line hamburger menu icon in white, a gold four-point sparkle icon #C9A227, a red-crimson stylised 'T' logo #E8174F centred, a white magnifying-glass search icon at right, and a grey-and-white circular user avatar silhouette at far right. Beneath the bar is the article page on a white background: a small crimson monospace kicker 'Applied AI', a large serif headline, and a serif body text block with the first phrase in bold. The photo is not cropped inside the window; the image box is 1372 by 1684 pixels at x 57.1, y 11.0, w 35.7, h 78.0 and the visible window sits inside it with white margin. Lighting is flat, no overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background: Split: left half solid #F2F2F2 and right half solid #FFFFFF. Left half background #F2F2F2 with a flat right half #FFFFFF; no gradients. Quote text is grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif with regular weight, around 5.6% of slide height for the quote. Footer text is #A6A6A6: author name in MessinaSans-Bold, date and page number in MessinaSans-Book, about 1.9% of slide height. Screenshot text: kicker crimson #E8174F in a monospace face; headline and body in a Noto Serif-like serif with the headline near 2.8% of slide height and body near 1.8%. Body text colour black #111111 in the screenshot. Window has a 2% of slide height corner radius, soft grey drop shadow below and around it, and a 1 px light grey border. No accent lines or other shapes.

### Slide 21

**Text**

History doesn’t repeat, but it rhymes
Press reports: “Usage surge from a new application overwhelms infrastructure and drives new pricing models”
The New York Times
GIVE THE TIMES
Heaviest Users of Phone Data Will Pay More
Share full article
By Matt Richtel
June 2, 2010
They spend hours watching video on their phones, downloading songs, browsing the Web, sending photos to friends and generally using mobile devices as full-fledged computers. They are the data hogs.
On Wednesday, AT&T pulled away the trough. And other wireless carriers could do the same.
AT&T said it would no longer offer an unlimited data plan to new users of iPhones and other smartphones. The decision, industry analysts said, could signal a shift away from an era in which American wireless carriers sought to attract customers with simple, all-you-can-eat pricing plans for data.
The trouble for AT&T was that a fraction of users fewer than 2
The Information
In-depth insights in seconds. Ask Deep Research.
Exclusive
Anthropic Changes Pricing to Bill Firms Based on AI Use as Demand Jumps
Art by Mike Sullivan.
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
21

**Layout**

Title sits top-left at about 5.6% from the top with a subtitle quote beneath it, both left-aligned on a shared left edge near x 20.5%. Two browser-window mockups sit side by side in the lower two-thirds, left card at x 17.7% and right card at x 63.4%, each about 36% wide and 78% tall, with a grey arrow between them at about 57% x. Footer line sits at about 91% from the top, right-aligned to the slide edge at about 96%, with name, dash-separated date and slide number 21.

**Visuals**

Two browser-window mockups with three traffic-light dots (red, yellow, green) at top-left of each white card. Left mockup is a screenshot of a New York Times article page: masthead wordmark, hamburger and account icons, the headline in bold italic serif, a 'Share full article' pill with gift icon, a share arrow icon and a bookmark icon, byline, date, and body text cut off at the bottom edge. Right mockup is a screenshot of The Information article page: hamburger icon, masthead, avatar icon, a full-width black banner with a red arrow, a red 'Exclusive' kicker, a headline, and a cut-out illustration. The illustration shows a cyan (#00D8E8) grid background with a coral-orange (#F0A070) paper cut-out of a figure in a dark navy suit whose head is replaced by a coral starburst; a hand from the lower left holds out a green banknote toward the figure's open hand. The cut-out has a white outline and a drop shadow, is full-width within the card and boxed, and is cropped at the bottom edge. A grey (#A6A6A6) solid right-pointing triangle sits between the cards, indicating progression from left to right.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat light grey #F1F1F1 fill covering the entire slide. Flat light grey background with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle in dark grey #545454 in a high-contrast Modern serif (MessinaModern-Regular); title is about 5.6% of slide height and subtitle about 2.6%. Footer name in bold sans #A6A6A6, date and number in book-weight sans #A6A6A6, about 1.9%. Mockup cards are white with rounded corners of about 2% radius and a soft grey drop shadow. Article body text is dark serif (#333333), headlines near-black #121212, the NYT masthead black, The Information red kicker #E8174B, black banner #000000 with white text, caption in grey monospace #666666. Arrow is flat grey #A6A6A6 with no outline.

### Slide 22

**Text**

Mobile networks in 2010 = LLMs in 2026?
How do you manage explosive demand with marginal cost and limited capacity? And then what?
Bits = tokens
Marginal cost
Opaque & poorly
aligned with use
cases or value
Equilibrium
Flat-rate capped
bundles align
price, usage &
capacity growth
Wifi/Edge
Now v. next
Supply/demand
imbalance today lets
labs price at ROI
But long-term
pricing power is
unlikely
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
22

**Layout**

Single row of three equal white circles spanning the middle of the slide, left-to-right, with grey triangular arrows between them. Title is centred near the top at about 5.6% from the top, with a smaller centred subtitle directly beneath it; the footer line (author, date, page number) sits at the bottom right at about 91% from the top, with the page number at the far right margin. Left margin is empty; content is centred on the slide's horizontal axis.

**Visuals**

Three white circles with dark grey (#545454) outlines about 0.3% of slide width thick, each about 21.7% wide by 38.5% tall, sitting at y 34.3%. The third circle has a red outline (about #E84B3C) instead of grey. Between circles 1 and 2, and between 2 and 3, is a small solid grey (#A6A6A6) right-pointing triangle, about 3% wide and 10% tall, vertically centred on the circles. Circle 1 contains bold heading 'Bits = tokens' followed by 'Marginal cost' and 'Opaque & poorly aligned with use cases or value'. Circle 2 contains bold heading 'Equilibrium' followed by 'Flat-rate capped bundles align price, usage & capacity growth' and 'Wifi/Edge'. Circle 3 contains bold heading 'Now v. next' followed by 'Supply/demand imbalance today lets labs price at ROI' and 'But long-term pricing power is unlikely'. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Plain light grey background (#F1F1F1) with no gradient or pattern. Circles are white (#FFFFFF) filled with 2-point-ish outlines; no shadows, no corner radii (circular). Arrows are flat with no outline.

**Text**

And so far, models do seem to be commodities / For most general and consumer use, models are very similar, and crucially there are no network effects / Selected frontier LLMs by aggregate benchmark score / OpenAI / Anthropic / Google / Meta / Chinese labs / 70 / 60 / 50 / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / Jun 2022 / Jun 2023 / Jun 2024 / Jun 2025 / Jun 2026 / Source: ArtificialAnalysis / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 25

**Layout**

Full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band across the top with a white content panel below it and a light grey footer band at the bottom. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 20.5% in a serif face, with the chart title, legend and plot starting at the same left edge; the legend sits at the far left (x 4.8%) outside the plot. The source line sits at the left edge of the footer (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number are right-aligned at the footer's right side, with the page number at about x 94.3%.

**Visuals**

Chart: scatter plot titled 'Selected frontier LLMs by aggregate benchmark score'. Y axis: 'aggregate benchmark score' with no unit printed, range 0 to 70, ticks every 10 (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70), dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at each tick, solid thin grey baseline at 0. X axis: time from Jun 2022 to Jun 2026, labelled at Jun 2022, Jun 2023, Jun 2024, Jun 2025 and Jun 2026. Series, shown as filled circles of about 1.5% of slide width: OpenAI red #E8473A; Anthropic black #000000; Google dark grey #555555; Meta mid grey #9E9E9E; Chinese labs light grey #D9D9D9. Legend at left, top to bottom: OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Meta, Chinese labs, each with a small filled dot of its series colour. No values are printed on points; approximate values follow. OpenAI: about Dec 2022 9; about Mar 2023 13; about Nov 2023 14; about Jan 2024 15; about Mar 2024 19; about Apr 2024 24; about Oct 2024 31; about Dec 2024 38; about Jan 2025 41; about Mar 2025 45; about May 2025 48; about Jun 2025 51; about Aug 2025 54; about Sep 2025 57; about Oct 2025 60. Meta: about Mar 2023 7.5; about Jun 2023 9.5 and about 8.5; about Mar 2024 18; about Oct 2024 19; about Sep 2025 52. Chinese labs: about Mar 2023 7.5; about Nov 2023 9; about Jan 2024 9.5 and 12; about Jun 2024 16 and 17; about Oct 2024 19 to 22.5; about Dec 2024 27; about Feb 2025 29.5; about Mar 2025 31 and 34; about Jul 2025 41 to 42.5; about Aug 2025 47 and 50; about Sep 2025 54. **Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF middle panel covering the slide from about y 20.5% to 86%. Two-tone layout: light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white middle panel. LLMs

Commodity?
Low margin?
Will LLMs have
pricing power?
Can they capture
use-cases & value
further up the
stack?
Benedict Evans –– May 2026 26

**Layout**

Three white circles of equal size sit in a single horizontal row across the middle-lower area of the slide, separated by two small grey arrow chevrons pointing right; the title sits top-left at about x 20.5% with a subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned on a shared left edge. The footer line (author name, date, page number) sits bottom-right at about y 91%, with the page number at far right, leaving a wide empty margin on the left.

Three circles with thin dark grey outlines (about #545454, about 0.3% of slide height stroke) filled white, arranged left to right; the first two have dark grey outlines and the third has a red outline (about #E84C3D, same stroke width). Between circle 1 and circle 2 and between circle 2 and circle 3 sit two solid grey right-pointing triangular chevrons (about #A6A6A6), each about 2.5% wide and 6% tall, vertically centred on the circles. Circle 1 contains, centred: 'Software' (bold) then 'Capital-light', 'Network effects', 'Monopolies', 'High margin'. Circle 2 contains, centred: 'LLMs' (bold) then 'Capital-intensive', 'No network effects?', 'Commodity?', 'Low margin?'. Circle 3 contains, centred: 'Will LLMs have / pricing power?' then 'Can they capture / use-cases & value / further up the / stack?'. No photographs, logos or charts.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide.

### Slide 27

**Text**

A provisional thesis / This is all very early, and these might be the wrong answers, but hopefully they’re the right questions / Chat is a / terrible UX / General use / needs ‘apps’ / Labs can’t / build (or / generate) all / the apps / Models are / commodities / and have no / network / effects / Models will / just be infra / Innovation / will move up / the stack / Benedict Evans –– May 2026 / 27

**Layout**

Four equal circles sit in a single horizontal row across the centre-lower area of the slide, joined by three small right-pointing triangles, with the left margin of the title and subtitle at about 20.5% of slide width. The title sits top-left at about 5.6% from the top, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it. The footer (author name, dash, date) and the slide number sit bottom-right on one baseline at about 91% from the top; the slide number is right-aligned near the right edge.

**Visuals**

Four solid circles in a row, each about 16.2% of slide width and 28.8% of slide height, with white serif text centred inside. Circles 1 to 3 are dark grey #535353; circle 4 at the right is red #EB483F. Three small right-pointing isosceles triangles in light grey #A6A6A6 sit in the gaps between circles, each about 1.9% of width by 6.9% of height, vertically centred on the circles, with no outline and no shadow. No photographs, logos or icons. No chart or table.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat fill #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat light grey background #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title in MessinaModern-Regular (a serif, book-weight face) at about 5.56% of slide height, colour #545454. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular at about 2.59% of slide height, colour #545454. Circle text in MessinaModern-Book (serif, regular weight) at about 2.96% of slide height, white #FFFFFF, centred, with line spacing close to 1.0 and small gaps between paragraphs. Footer in MessinaSans-Bold for the name and MessinaSans-Book for the date and number, at about 1.85% of slide height, colour #A6A6A6. Circles are plain filled ellipses with no outline, shadow or gradient. Arrows are plain filled triangles with no outline. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a common left edge.

Single full-bleed dark slide with a large lighter rectangular panel inset from the left and right, with the title placed at the top-left inside the panel. A thin white horizontal rule sits along the bottom edge of the panel. The footer line sits below the panel at the bottom right, with the author name, date and slide number in a single row, and the slide number at the far right margin.

none

Footer text in MessinaSans, about 1.85% of slide height, grey #A6A6A6: author name in Bold, date and slide number in Book weight. The dash mark between name and date is a double en-dash rendered in grey. Thin white rule about 0.3% of height along the bottom edge of the panel, spanning its full width. NB: round numbers reported without precise dates / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 29

**Layout**

Full-width two-band layout: a light grey header band (top 20.5% of the slide) holds the title and subtitle left-aligned at x about 20.5%, and a white middle band (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holds the line chart, which spans about x 19.6% to 87.7%. A light grey footer band (y 86.1% to 100%) holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number ending at x about 95.5%.

**Visuals**

One line chart, titled 'ChatGPT global weekly active users (m)', with a y-axis from 0 to 1,000 (units: millions of weekly active users; labelled ticks at 0, 250, 500, 750, 1,000) and an x-axis of dates labelled Dec 2022, Jun 2023, Dec 2023, Jun 2024, Dec 2024, Jun 2025, Dec 2025, Jun 2026 (half-year intervals). The chart has no legend because it has a single series, drawn as a smooth red line (about #EE3F35) 2 px wide with round red circular markers at each data point. Values printed on the slide: none; the values below are read from the marker positions and are estimates: Dec 2022 about 0; about Nov 2023 about 100; about Aug 2024 about 200; about Oct 2024 about 300; about Feb 2025 about 400; about Jun 2025 about 700; about Oct 2025 about 800; about Dec 2025 about 900. Gridlines are light grey dotted horizontal lines at 250, 500, 750 and 1,000; the x-axis baseline at 0 is a thin solid grey line. No points are highlighted beyond the markers themselves. The chart ends at the last marker, about Dec 2025, with no further data to Jun 2026.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 across the header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart. Quiet, minimal editorial look on a light grey and white field. Titles are set in a serif (Messina Modern Regular, a transitional-style serif, roughly 5.6% of slide height) in dark grey #545454. Body and subtitle text is serif (Messina Modern Regular, about 2.6% of slide height) in #545454. Chart labels and footer use a geometric sans-serif (Messina Sans Regular, about 1.85% of slide height) in #545454 and #535353. Author name is Messina Sans Bold in #A6A6A6, date and page number are Messina Sans Book in #A6A6A6. Accent colour is red #EE3F35 for the data line and markers. Chart gridlines are light grey dotted hairlines. No shadows, gradients, corner radii or boxes.

Consumer use is a mile wide and an inch deep

Lowest observed

Benedict Evans

30

**Layout**

Two full-width horizontal bands frame the slide: a light grey header band (top 0 to about 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle, and a light grey footer band (from about 86.1% to the bottom) holding the source line, author, date and page number. A white content band sits between them, where the chart fills the width from about x 19.6 to 95.5, with the legend at the far left (x 4.8). The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x about 20.5, the source line is left-aligned at x 20.5, and the author, date and page number are right-side footer items ending at about x 95.5. The page number is the far-right footer item.

**Visuals**

Line chart with markers. Title above plot: 'Range of user-reported total ChatGPT messages sent in 2025, by cohort'. Y-axis: messages sent in 2025 (count), linear scale from 0 to 5,000 with dotted horizontal gridlines every 1,000 and labelled ticks at 0, 1,000, 2,000, 3,000, 4,000, 5,000. X-axis: categories Top 5%, Top 10%, Top 15%, Top 20% (cohort percentile), with a solid grey baseline at 0. Series 1 'Highest observed', dark grey line with dark grey circular markers, values about 4,350 (Top 5%), about 2,600 (Top 10%), about 1,800 (Top 15%), about 1,450 (Top 20%). Series 2 'Lowest observed', red line with red circular markers, values about 2,900 (Top 5%), about 2,000 (Top 10%), about 1,500 (Top 15%), about 1,200 (Top 20%). Lines are smoothed curves. Legend at upper left of the plot: dark grey dot-line marker 'Highest observed' above red dot-line marker 'Lowest observed'. No highlighted points beyond the markers. No photographs or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 with a white #FFFFFF band from about y 20.5 to 86.1. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF content band between them. Title in a serif modern face (MessinaModern-Regular, a high-contrast serif similar to Century-style book serif), regular weight, colour #545454, large (about 5.6% of slide height). Subtitle in the same serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis/legend labels in a geometric grotesque sans (MessinaSans family): chart title SemiBold #545454 about 2.2%, labels Regular #535353 to #545454 about 1.85%. Footer: author name Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number Book #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. Source line Regular #545454. Accent colours: dark grey #545454 for the Highest observed series and red #EE4B3B for the Lowest observed series. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey. Baseline is a thin solid grey line. No shadows, gradients or corner radii.

**Layout**

Single-column slide with a full-width light grey header band holding a left-aligned title and subtitle, a white body band with the chart, and a grey footer band. The title block starts at about 20.5% of slide width and the chart plot spans from about 20.5% to 95.3%, so the left margin is about 20.5% with a right margin of about 4.7%. Source line sits bottom-left at the same left edge as the title, and the author, date and page number sit bottom-right, with the page number at about 94.3% x.

**Visuals**

Stacked area chart (drawn as filled bands per industry, each band running from a left edge to a right edge) titled US workplace AI use by industry, June 2023 to December 2025. Y axis: percentage of respondents, 0% to 100%, gridlines and labels at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%. X axis: seven industries, Tech, Finance, Professional services, Healthcare, Retail, Manufacturing, Government. Three stacked series from bottom to top: Daily (red #EB483F), Weeky (dark grey #535353), Any (light grey #A7A7A7). No values are printed; estimates from the left and right edge of each band are given as about values, in the order Daily / Weekly top / Any top. Tech left: about 7 / 20 / 38; right: about 31 / 57 / 77. Finance left: about 4 / 10 / 23; right: about 19 / 40 / 64. Professional services left: about 3 / 9 / 26; right: about 16 / 36 / 62. Healthcare left: about 6 / 11 / 17; right: about 13 / 25 / 41. Retail left: about 8 / 18 / 26; right: about 10 / 19 / 33. Manufacturing left: about 6 / 9 / 18; right: about 9 / 20 / 41. Government left: about 2 / 8 / 18; right: about 8 / 21 / 42. Legend at top-left of plot: Any, Weeky, Daily with square swatches in the same colours. No highlighted points. Dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at 25, 50, 75 and 100 percent; solid thin grey baseline at 0%.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF body band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands framing a white (#FFFFFF) body band. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title in MessinaSans-SemiBold sans-serif, colour #545554, about 2.2%. Axis and category labels in MessinaSans-Regular sans-serif, colour #535353 or #545554, about 1.85%. Footer text in MessinaSans, #A6A6A6, with Benedict Evans in Bold and the date and page number in Book. Accent colour red #EB483F for Daily. Series fills are flat with no gradients, shadows or corner radii; thin white edges separate the bands.

### Slide 33

**Text**

Imagine you were an / accountant seeing the first / software spreadsheets / Now imagine you were a / lawyer: “very cool, but…” / Source: Computer History Museum / Benedict Evans / –– August 2026 / 33

**Layout**

Two-column split. The left half is a light grey text column with left-aligned text starting at x 4.6%; the statement sits in the vertical middle (about y 25% to 65%) and the source line sits near the bottom-left (y 90.9%). The right half is a full-bleed black-and-white photograph running to the top, right and bottom slide edges, with the footer row (author, date, page number) along the bottom of the photo at y 91.1%. No title, no visible slide-number box apart from the footer '33'.

**Visuals**

One black-and-white photograph occupying the right half, full-bleed (x 46.8% to 100%, y 0% to 100%), cropped at the right and bottom edges. Subject: an Apple II personal computer (beige case with a rainbow-style 'apple II' badge on the lower front panel, detachable keyboard, separate beige CRT monitor) displaying a spreadsheet on its screen, with the header 'FINANCIAL HIGHLIGHT', 'ABC COMPUTER, INC', 'FOR THE FISCAL YEARS ENDING SEPTEMBER', 'DOLLARS AND SHARES IN THOUSANDS', column headings '1982' and '1983', and row labels including NET SALES, NET INCOME, EARNINGS PER SHARE, PRE-TAX RETURN ON NET SALE, EFFECTIVE TAX RATE, RETURN ON NET SALE, CASH AND CASH EQUIVALENTS and ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE, with numeric values in the two year columns. At the far right edge, partly cropped, are two stacked Disk II floppy drives labelled '1' and '2' and 'disk II'. Composition: computer shown three-quarter view from the front-left, centred slightly right of the photo, on a dark grey tabletop. Lighting: soft studio light from above-left; background is a dark grey gradient, darkest at top-right and lighter behind the monitor. Dominant colours: greyscale only, with off-white/light grey hardware and near-black screen. No overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F2F2F2 across the left half, with a white #FFFFFF right half behind the full-bleed photograph. Light grey background (#F2F2F2) on the left with a white (#FFFFFF) right half that the photo covers. Statement text in a serif typeface, MessinaModern-Regular, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height per line (line pitch about 6.7% of slide height), colour #545454. Source line and footer in MessinaSans-Book (sans-serif), about 1.85% of slide height, colour #545454 for the source and #A6A6A6 for the footer. 'Benedict Evans' is set in MessinaSans-Bold, the date and page number in MessinaSans-Book. Lines and shapes: none. No corner radii, shadows or gradients on shapes; the photo has no border or overlay.

### Slide 34

**Text**

“What’s this for?” Well, what’s the pattern? / How do we always deploy new technologies? / Absorb / Doing the old things better with the new technology / Innovate? / Things that are only possible with the new technology / Disrupt? / Redefine the question / Benedict Evans –– May 2026 34

**Layout**

Three equal white circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle-lower slide, joined by two small grey chevrons, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about 20.5% from the left edge near the top. The title sits above the circles, the subtitle sits below it, and a footer line with author, date and page number runs along the bottom right at about 91% height.

**Visuals**

Diagram: three white circles with thin dark grey outlines (#545454, about 0.3% of slide width stroke) arranged left to right, the third circle outlined in red. Circle 1 contains the bold heading 'Absorb' and body text 'Doing the old things better with the new technology'. Circle 2 contains 'Innovate?' and 'Things that are only possible with the new technology'. Circle 3 (red outline, about #E74C3C) contains 'Disrupt?' and 'Redefine the question'. Between circle 1 and 2, and between circle 2 and 3, is a solid grey right-pointing triangle (chevron) in #A6A6A6 connecting them in sequence. No photographs, logos or icons; no chart or table.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat light grey background #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title and body text in dark grey #545454; footer text in lighter grey #A6A6A6. Typeface is a transitional/Scotch-style serif (MessinaModern family) in Regular, Book and SemiBold weights for titles, circle headings and body; the footer uses a sans-serif (MessinaSans) in Bold and Book. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and circle headings about 2.6% and 3.3%, circle body text about 3.0%, footer about 1.9%. Circles are plain white fills with no shadow, no rounded corners beyond the perfect circle form, and the chevrons are plain flat grey triangles with no outline.

### Slide 35

**Text**

What works first?
First, automate the obvious use cases - innovation takes longer

Results in 2025
Expected in 2026

Easy to deploy, hard to measure
3%
Hard to deploy, easy to measure
2%

0%
Productivity
Better insights &
decision-making
Better customer
service
Cost-saving
New
revenue
Source: Atlanta Fed: Baslandze et al
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
35

**Layout**

Three horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle left-aligned at x 20.5%; a white content band (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holding the chart, which runs from a legend at the far left to a plot area spanning x 20.5% to 95.2%; a light grey footer band (below 86.1%) with the source line left-aligned at x 20.5% and the author, date and page number right-aligned near x 94.3%. The page number 35 sits at the bottom right, at x 94.3%, y 91.1%.

**Visuals**

Chart: grouped vertical bar chart titled 'Impact of AI deployment (US CFOs surveyed in December 2025)'. Y-axis: percentage, 0% to 4%, gridlines at every 1% (dotted light grey), labels 0%, 1%, 2%, 3%, 4%. X-axis: five categories, labelled Productivity, Better insights & decision-making, Better customer service, Cost-saving, New revenue. Two series: 'Results in 2025' in red #EB483F and 'Expected in 2026' in dark grey #535353. Values: Productivity 2025 about 1.8% (printed value not shown), 2026 about 3.0%; Better insights & decision-making 2025 about 1.9%, 2026 about 3.3%; Better customer service 2025 about 0.8%, 2026 about 2.1%; Cost-saving 2025 about 0.1%, 2026 no bar shown; New revenue 2025 about 0.5%, 2026 about 1.3%. No values are printed on the bars; all values are estimates. Legend at top left of the chart with red square for 'Results in 2025' and dark grey square for 'Expected in 2026'. Two unboxed annotations in the plot: 'Easy to deploy, hard to measure' placed above the Productivity and Better insights bars, and 'Hard to deploy, easy to measure' placed above the Cost-saving and New revenue area. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the chart band. Title in a serif face (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a modern serif such as Georgia-like transitional) in grey #545454, regular weight, size about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, size about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title, axis labels, legend, category labels and annotations in a geometric sans-serif (MessinaSans, similar to a grotesque sans), regular weight; chart title is SemiBold #545554 at about 2.2%; body and axis labels about 1.85% of slide height. Source line in sans regular #545454 at about 1.85%. Footer author name in sans Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in sans Book #A6A6A6, about 1.85%. Accent colour red #EB483F; primary data grey #535353. Gridlines dotted, thin, light grey. Baseline solid thin grey line at 0%. Bars are flat with no corner radius, no shadows, no gradients, with small white gaps between paired bars.

### Slide 36

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: Cut or merge the sparse quote slides (50, 51, 60) and vary the circle diagrams so that slides 36, 42 and 44 carry more than short phrases in circles.

**Text**

What’s working first? / Where is it easy and obvious to use generative AI? / Analytics & / productivity / Marketing / Customer / support / Back-office / processes / Coding! / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 36

**Layout**

Four equal circles sit in one horizontal row across the middle-lower area, spaced evenly and aligned on a shared centre line, with the three dark circles followed by the red one at the right. The title sits top-left at about x 20.5% and y 5.6%, with a smaller subtitle directly beneath it left-aligned to the same edge; the footer line sits bottom-right, with the name, date and page number on one baseline.

**Visuals**

Four solid filled circles, identical size (about 16.2% of slide width, 28.8% of slide height, i.e. a circle of roughly 325 by 325 px on a 2000 by 1125 canvas). Circles 1 to 3 are dark grey #535353 with white serif labels; circle 4 is red #EB483F with the white label 'Coding!'. No chart, photo, logo or icon. No connecting lines or arrows between circles.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat colour #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat light-grey background #F1F1F1 with no gradient, texture or shadow. Title and subtitle in #545454 MessinaModern-Regular serif, title about 5.6% of slide height and subtitle about 2.6%. Circle labels in white #FFFFFF MessinaModern-Book serif at about 3.0% of slide height. Footer in #A6A6A6 MessinaSans: 'Benedict Evans' Bold and '–– May 2026' and page number in Book weight at about 1.9%. Circles are flat with no outline, no corner radius (they are circles), no shadow. Accent colour is the single red #EB483F circle.

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band across the top (about 0 to 20.5% of slide height) holding a serif title left-aligned at about x 20.5% with a subtitle beneath it; a white content band fills the middle (20.5% to 86.1%) containing a single vertical bar chart whose left edge sits at about x 19% (y-axis labels) and whose plot runs to about x 95%; a light grey footer band below (86.1% to 100%) holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number right-aligned toward x 95%.

**Visuals**

Vertical bar chart titled 'Annualised enterprise AI spending, March 2026 ($bn)'. Y-axis: labelled 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 with units $bn implied by the title, range 0 to 4, light dotted horizontal gridlines at each integer. X-axis: categories Coding, Legal, Support, Medical & health admin, Search, Text, Real estate, Finance, baseline solid thin grey line at 0. Series values with no printed data labels, so estimates: Coding about 3.0 (highlighted red bar), Legal about 0.5, Support about 0.4, Medical & health admin about 0.35, Search about 0.25, Text about 0.15, Real estate about 0.15, Finance about 0.05. Bars are dark grey #515151 except Coding which is red #EB483F and is the only highlighted point. No legend. Bars are evenly spaced, approximately 60 px wide each at slide width of 2000 px, with rectangular square corners.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the chart band. Title text in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 5.56% of slide height for the title with the subtitle about 2.59%. Chart and footer text in MessinaSans sans-serif, regular weight, #545554 for the chart text, the axis labels about 1.85% of slide height; chart title MessinaSans-SemiBold about 2.22%. Footer author name bold #A6A6A6, date and page number book weight #A6A6A6. Accent colour red #EB483F used only on the Coding bar. No shadows, gradients, or rounded corners; thin dotted gridlines in grey.

**Text**

How do you build ‘AI software’?
How do you turn an amazing raw technology into something people can use?
“Blank screen”
“Jagged frontier”
Use cases!
Tooling, GUI,
process, GTM,
proprietary data,
workflows
Does the model
go at the top of
the stack or the
bottom?
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
44

**Layout**

Single row of three equal circles centred vertically in the body area, separated by two small chevron arrows, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top and a footer line at bottom right. The title starts at about 20.5% from the left and the footer ends at about 95.6%, leaving wide empty margins on both sides; there is no grid or column rule beyond the three-circle row.

**Visuals**

Diagram: three filled circles of equal size (each about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height) in a horizontal row, left to right. Circle 1 is dark grey #535353 and contains the two quotations “Blank screen” and “Jagged frontier” in white serif type. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353 and contains the heading Use cases! followed by the list Tooling, GUI, process, GTM, proprietary data, workflows in white serif type. Circle 3 is red #EB483F and contains the question Does the model go at the top of the stack or the bottom? in white serif type. Between each pair of circles is a small right-pointing solid triangle (chevron arrow) in mid grey #A6A6A6, about 2% of slide width and 6.8% of slide height, vertically centred on the circles, showing flow from circle 1 to circle 2 and from circle 2 to circle 3. No photographs, logos or charts.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Flat, minimal style on a light grey background. Background #F1F1F1. Title and subtitle in grey #545454 MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight. Circle text in white #FFFFFF MessinaModern-Book serif. Footer in #A6A6A6, with the author name in MessinaSans-Bold and the date and page number in MessinaSans-Book sans-serif. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle text about 3.3%, footer about 1.9%. Shapes are plain solid fills with no outlines, no shadows and no gradients; circles are perfect circles and the arrows are plain triangles.

45

**Layout**

Single-column layout on a three-band structure: a grey header band (0 to 20.5% of slide height) holding the title and subtitle, a white central band (20.5% to 86.1%) holding the chart, and a grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.5%, as is the chart title and the source line; the chart's y-axis labels sit at about x 17% and the chart plot runs from about x 20.5% to 95.4%. The right edge of the footer items and the plot is about 95.4% to 96.5%, leaving right margin about 3.5%. Slide number sits at the bottom right, x 94.3%, y 91.1%.

**Visuals**

One line chart, placed as a single image of 1465 by 453 pixels at x 19.6%, y 40.0%, w 76.3%, h 41.9%. Chart type: line chart with circular markers and a smoothed (curved) line. Chart title printed above the plot: 'Accenture reported new quarterly 'generative AI' contracts ($m)'. Y-axis: 'contracts ($m)' scale from 0 to 2,500 with labels 0, 500, 1,000, 1,500, 2,000, 2,500 (unit $m); horizontal dotted light-grey gridlines at each 500 step; solid grey baseline at 0. ### Slide 46

**Text**

Automation takes a lot of manual labour
PE roll-ups and GTM partnerships with outsourcers and strategy consultants
OpenAI
Today, we're announcing our Frontier Alliances. Boston Consulting Group (BCG) and McKinsey & Company as well as Accenture and Capgemini will help customers define strategy, integrate systems, redesign workflows, and scale deployment globally. We're entering multi-year partnerships with each firm to help deploy AI coworkers across the enterprise.
Our partners will work alongside OpenAI's Forward Deployed Engineering (FDE) team, combining OpenAI's research and product expertise with deep transformation experience and global delivery teams. Each partner is investing in dedicated practice groups and building teams that will be certified on OpenAI technology. OpenAI will support them with technical resources, roadmap insight, and access to our product and research teams.
Experts in strategy, AI, and change management
McKinsey and BCG each bring deep experience to help leadership decide how to start, redesign their operating model, embed AI, and drive adoption.

The goal of both efforts is to accelerate adoption of AI technology and boost sales, with OpenAI and Anthropic targeting initial public offerings as soon as this year.

**Visuals**

One chart: a grouped stacked column chart, with 10 categories (Software, Customer Service, Marketing, Knowledge Management, Operations, IT, Sales, Finance, HR, Legal). Each category has three side-by-side columns, each column stacked with a red Production segment at the bottom and a dark grey Development/Pilot segment on top. The slide prints no values; estimates are marked about. Y-axis: 0% to 80%, labelled every 10%, with light dotted gridlines at each 10% and a solid grey baseline at 0%. The x-axis has no unit label; the slide does not label which of the three columns in each category corresponds to Q4 2023, Q3 2024 or Q3 2025. ### Slide 48

**Text**

Startups exist to unbundle use cases / The coming wave of AI startups trying to unbundle Google, Excel, email and Oracle… and ChatGPT / Y Combinator startups by field / Other / 500 / AI / 400 / 300 / 200 / 100 / 0 / W 2015 / W 2016 / W 2017 / W 2018 / W 2019 / W 2020 / W 2021 / W 2022 / W 2023 / W 2024 / F 2024 / S 2025 / Source: Y Combinator / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 48

**Layout**

Two bands: a full-width light grey header band across the top about 20% of the slide height holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at x 20.5% (about one fifth in from the left edge), and a full-width white body from y 20.5% to 86% holds a legend at the far left and the chart from x 20% to 95%. A grey footer band below y 86% holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and slide number at right, with the slide number at x 94.3%, roughly 5.7% margin from the right edge.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical stacked column chart titled 'Y Combinator startups by field'. Y axis: number of startups, 0 to 500, gridlines at 0, 100, 200, 300, 400 and 500 (dotted light grey horizontal lines, labels at left). X axis: time periods, with labels W 2015, W 2016, W 2017, W 2018, W 2019, W 2020, W 2021, W 2022, W 2023, W 2024, F 2024, S 2025 under every second column; 24 columns in total, the unlabelled columns sit between labelled ones. Two series stacked: bottom series 'AI' in red, top series 'Other' in dark grey. No values are printed on the slide, so all values are estimates marked 'about'. Column estimates (AI / Other total): W 2015 about 20 / 110; unlabelled column 2 about 16 / 104; W 2016 about 21 / 121; column 4 about 19 / 101; W 2017 about 23 / 116; column 6 about 21 / 124; W 2018 about 26 / 146; column 8 about 32 / 131; W 2019 about 44 / 194; column 10 about 46 / 175; W 2020 about 68 / 228; column 12 about 57 / 206; W 2021 about 95 / 336; column 14 about 131 / 391; W 2022 about 172 / 399; column 16 about 125 / 234; W 2023 about 176 / 276; column 18 about 169 / 220; W 2024 about 201 / 251; column 20 about 213 / 254; F 2024 about 80 / 93; column 22 about 143 / 166; S 2025 about 130 / 142; column 24 about 145 / 167. Legend at top-left of chart area: dark grey square labelled Other above a red square labelled AI. No highlighted points; columns have a thin white gap at the boundary between series. Dark grey bars are the Other series and red bars are AI.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF body band. Title colour warm dark grey #545454, subtitle #545454. Chart title and axis labels #545454. Footer source #545454; author name #A6A6A6 bold, date and page number #A6A6A6 light. Accent red #EA4A3C for AI series, dark grey #545454 for Other series. Title typeface MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, title about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle MessinaModern-Regular about 2.6%. Chart and footer typeface MessinaSans (Regular, SemiBold for chart title, Bold for author name, Book for date), a humanist sans-serif, about 1.9% of slide height for labels and 2.2% for chart title. Shapes are flat with no corner radii, shadows or gradients. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey lines. Column bars are rectangles with square corners.

**Review notes**

- Review: The deck has a clear route of Capital, Deployment and Change (slides 2, 3, 28, 49), and most chart slides pair a plain headline with a subtitle that states the point, such as slide 8 with its comparison to telecoms and oil and gas.

**Text**

Change
Benedict Evans –– May 2026 49

**Layout**

Single-title section slide. A large panel covers the central-right area of the slide (from about 17% to 95% horizontally and 20% to 81% vertically), with the title 'Change' placed in its upper-left area at about 20% from the left edge. A thin white horizontal rule runs along the bottom edge of the panel at about 81% height. The footer sits along the bottom right, with the author name, dash and date, and the slide number at the far right margin. The left margin outside the panel is empty.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid dark slate colour #36494E. Dark slate background #36494E with a slightly lighter translucent white panel (white at 4% opacity over the background, appearing about #3E5055). Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, white #FFFFFF, roughly 9.3% of slide height, regular weight, left aligned. Footer in MessinaSans-Bold for the author name and MessinaSans-Book for the date and page number, grey #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height. Thin white horizontal rule across the bottom of the panel, about 0.3% of slide height thick. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 50

**Text**

“It’s tough to make predictions, especially / about the future” / Yogi Berra / Benedict Evans –– May 2026 / 50

**Layout**

Single quote slide on a light grey field with a large white rectangular panel inset from the left and right, starting at about 17.4% from the left and running to about 95.6% of the width, and from 24.9% to 86.1% of the height. The quote is left-aligned inside the panel with its left edge at about 20.5% of the slide width, sitting in the upper part of the panel with the attribution below after a blank line. The footer sits bottom right beneath the panel: author name bold, then a double-dash and date, then the page number flush to the right margin at about 95.6% of the width.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 covering the entire slide. Light grey background #F2F2F2 with a white panel #FFFFFF with square corners and no visible shadow. Quote text and attribution are in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Book, a book-weight modern serif, approximately Didone-like) in red #EB483F, regular weight, approximately 5.6% of slide height for the lines. Footer uses a sans-serif (MessinaSans) in grey #A6A6A6, author name in bold, date and page number in book weight, about 1.85% of slide height. No accent shapes, lines or gradients.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

### Slide 52

**Text**

What can we automate with AI?
Adding probabilistic systems on top of deterministic systems as a new general purpose technology
Old
Anything we can describe in logical steps can be automated
New
Anything with enough training data
Anything where verification is scalable
Benedict Evans –– May 2026 52

**Layout**

Single-slide composition with no grid lines. The title sits top-left at x 20.5%, y 5.6%, with a subtitle line beneath it at y 13.3%, both left-aligned to roughly the same left edge. Two equal circles sit side by side in the central band (y 33.9% to 73.2%), centred at x 35.5% and x 70.5%, with a dotted vertical red divider at x 53% between them. The footer runs along y 91% at the bottom right: 'Benedict Evans' in bold, then '–– May 2026', then the page number 52 at x 94.3%. No margins are drawn; the left margin of the title is about 20.5%.

**Visuals**

Two solid filled circles and one vertical dotted line; no photographs, charts, logos or icons. Left circle: dark charcoal #535353, diameter about 22.1% of slide width, centred at x 35.5%, containing the white heading 'Old' and a four-line white body text. Right circle: signal red #EB483F, same size, centred at x 70.5%, containing the white heading 'New' and two paragraphs in white. Divider: a dotted vertical line in red #EB483F, about 0.3% wide, running from about y 31.6% to y 78.0% at x about 53.0%, with evenly spaced small dots. Circles are flat with no outline, no shadow and no gradient.

**Design**

Background flat light grey #F1F1F1 with no texture. Title and subtitle are in a Messina Modern-style book serif in grey #545454; the title is regular weight at about 5.6% of slide height (capitals about 3.9%), the subtitle about 2.6%. Circle headings are Messina Modern SemiBold in white #FFFFFF at about 3.3% of slide height; circle body text is Messina Modern Book in white at about 3.0%, centred, with line spacing about 1.25 times. Accent colours are charcoal #535353 and red #EB483F. Footer is a sans-serif in light grey #A6A6A6 at about 1.9% of slide height: 'Benedict Evans' bold, the rest book weight. Circles have fully rounded shapes, no corner radius, no shadow, no gradient.
