### Slide 53

**Text**

What does automation mean for you? It depends
At minimum, AI automates a broad class of thing that used to need a person. Then what?
Is this just / price elasticity / & consumer / surplus?
Which tasks / become free? / What does / that enable?
Was that cost / base your / moat?
What was / impossible / that’s now / cheap?
Benedict Evans –– May 2026 53

**Layout**

Four equal circles sit in a single horizontal row across the centre-right of the slide, evenly spaced, with the title left-aligned at top starting around 20.5% from the left and a subtitle beneath it. The footer sits at the bottom right with the author name, date and slide number on one baseline, and the slide number is right-aligned near the right margin.

**Visuals**

Four solid circles in a row, each about 16.2% of slide width and 28.8% of slide height, with centres evenly spaced at about 20% intervals. The first three circles are dark grey #535353 and the fourth circle, on the far right, is coral red #EB483F. Each circle holds white serif text centred inside it, asking a question. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is a flat light grey #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle are set in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif, in dark grey #545454; title is about 5.6% of slide height and subtitle about 2.6%. Circle text is MessinaModern-Book serif in white #FFFFFF at about 3.0% of slide height, with the fourth circle's text at about 3.3%. Footer is MessinaSans in light grey #A6A6A6 at about 1.9% of slide height, with 'Benedict Evans' in bold and the rest in book weight. Circles are plain filled shapes with no outline, no shadow and no corner radius (fully round).

### Slide 54

**Text**

Sometimes a job is just a task to be automated
Otis launched the ‘Autotronic’ automatic elevator in 1950, and this entire job became a button
Elevator attendants, USA
100,000
75,000
50,000
25,000
0
1900
1910
1920
1930
1940
1950
1960
1970
1980
1990
Source: US Census
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
54

**Layout**

Full-width grey header band across the top about 20.5% of the slide height holds a left-aligned serif title at x 20.5% with a serif subtitle beneath it. A white content panel fills the middle from y 20.5% to 86.1%, with a single line chart whose plot area starts at x about 20.5% and runs to about 95%. A grey footer band occupies the bottom 13.9%; the source line sits left at x 20.5% and the author, date and slide number sit right-aligned on the same baseline at about y 91%, with the slide number ending at x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

One line chart, titled 'Elevator attendants, USA'. Type: smoothed single-series line chart with no markers. Y axis: 0 to 100,000 people, labelled at 0, 25,000, 50,000, 75,000 and 100,000, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X axis: years 1900 to 1990, labelled every ten years (1900, 1910, 1920, 1930, 1940, 1950, 1960, 1970, 1980, 1990), with a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Series 'Elevator attendants' in red, values about: 1900 about 13,000; 1910 about 25,000; 1920 about 41,000; 1930 about 67,000; 1940 about 85,000; 1950 about 94,000 (peak); 1960 about 77,000; 1970 about 40,000; 1980 about 22,000; 1990 about 11,000. No legend, no data labels, no highlighted points beyond the peak near 1950 which is not marked. Values are estimates read from gridlines, not printed on the slide.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Two-tone flat layout: light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF middle panel. Title is a serif (MessinaModern Regular, a transitional-style serif) in dark grey #545454, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height for the title and about 2.6% for the subtitle, which is also serif #545454. Chart title and axis labels are a sans-serif (MessinaSans) in #545554; chart title SemiBold about 2.2%, axis labels Regular about 1.85%. Footer source text is sans #545454 regular about 1.85%; author name is sans Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number are sans Book #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. Data line is a red #E8453C stroke about 0.4% of slide height thick. Gridlines are thin dotted #BDBDBD. Baseline is a thin solid grey #8C8C8C line. No shadows, gradients, rounded corners or icons.

56

**Layout**

Full-bleed 16:9 slide split into three horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top 0 to about 20.5% of height) holding the title and subtitle, a white middle band (about 20.5% to 86.1%) holding the chart, and a light grey footer band (below about 86.1%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 20.5%, sharing a left edge with the chart caption and the source line; the chart spans from about x 16% to 96%. The page number sits at the far right of the footer, about 94% across.

**Visuals**

One line chart with markers. Type: line chart with circular markers, single series. Title: 'Accounting graduates hired by CPA firms, USA' (printed above the plot, left-aligned). Y axis: number of graduates, labelled 0 to 50,000 with gridlines at every 10,000 (0, 10,000, 20,000, 30,000, 40,000, 50,000), dotted light grey horizontal gridlines, no axis title printed. X axis: years 1970 to 2020, labels printed at 1970, 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2020; one point per year shown at irregular spacing in the early data and every second year after about 2000; solid grey baseline at 0. Legend: none. Series values (estimates, the slide prints no values): 1970 about 11,800; 1971 about 7,000; 1972 about 8,800; 1973 about 11,300; 1974 about 12,700; 1975 about 12,100; 1976 about 12,000; 1977 about 13,500; 1978 about 15,100; 1979 about 15,800; 1980 about 16,500; 1981 about 16,600; 1982 about 16,500; 1983 about 14,300; 1984 about 16,700; 1985 about 17,900; 1986 about 18,800; 1987 about 18,200; 1988 about 19,000; 1989 about 27,900 (the highest point before 2000); 1990 about 23,900; 1991 about 20,600; 1992 about 22,500; 1993 about 22,000; 1994 about 21,500; 1995 about 21,900; 1996 about 20,500; 1999 about 20,200; 2000 about 20,900; 2001 about 16,500; 2002 about 15,400; 2003 about 16,900; 2004 about 19,700; 2007 about 36,200; 2008 about 25,600; 2010 about 33,400; 2012 about 40,300; 2014 about 43,200 (the series peak); 2016 about 34,900; 2018 about 30,900; 2020 about 27,800. Highlighted points: none; all markers are the same size and colour. The line and markers are bright red-coral, about #EE4035, line width about 2 pt.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF middle band. Title colour dark grey #545454, subtitle colour dark grey #545454. Body and axis text dark grey #545454; footer author and date in light grey #A6A6A6. Accent colour is the chart red #EE4035. Title and subtitle are in a book-style high-contrast serif (closest match MessinaModern, serif, regular weight); title is about 5.6% of slide height and subtitle about 2.6%. Chart and footer text are a humanist sans-serif (closest match MessinaSans, sans-serif, regular and semibold weights), about 1.9% of slide height for axis and footer text and 2.2% for the chart caption. Lines are thin, dotted light grey gridlines and a solid grey baseline. No shadows, gradients, or rounded corners.

### Slide 57

**Text**

If the task becomes ‘free’ what does that unlock? / Automated inventory management let grocery stores stock 5x more products / Average SKUs per supermarket, USA / 60,000 / 50,000 / 40,000 / 30,000 / 20,000 / 10,000 / 0 / 1950 / 1960 / 1970 / 1980 / 1990 / 2000 / 2010 / 2020 / Grocery barcodes launched in 1974 / Source: FMI / Benedict Evans –– May 2026 / 57

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band across the top with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20.5%, a white chart panel in the middle band from y 20.5% to 86.1%, and a light grey footer band below with the source line at left and author, date and slide number right-aligned at the bottom. The chart plots from a y-axis at about x 20.6% to the right edge near x 95%, with the slide number at about x 94.4%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Average SKUs per supermarket, USA'. Y-axis: average SKUs per supermarket, from 0 to 60,000 in steps of 10,000 with dotted horizontal gridlines at each step. X-axis: years 1950 to 2024 with labels every 10 years (1950, 1960, ..., 2020). Single series drawn as a smoothed red line (about #E8473B) with approximate values: about 3,000 in 1955, about 6,000 in 1960, about 8,500 in 1974, about 14,500 in 1980, about 17,500 in 1985, about 26,000 in 1990, about 35,000 in 2003, peak about 51,000 in 2008, about 47,000 in 2010, about 39,000 in 2016, about 31,000 in 2020, about 32,000 in 2024. No values are printed on the line. A vertical dashed dark grey (#545454) line at 1974 marks the event, labelled 'Grocery barcodes launched in 1974' to its right. No legend. Highlighted point: the 1974 marker line and its label.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 with a white (#FFFFFF) central band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey (#F1F1F1) background with a white central chart band. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, large, approximately 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart heading in MessinaSans-SemiBold, #545554, about 2.2%. Axis and body labels in MessinaSans-Regular, #545454 or #535353, about 1.85%. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date and slide number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6, about 1.85%. Accent colour red line #E8473B. Gridlines thin dotted light grey. No shadows, gradients or corner radii.

Between them a vertical dotted line in red #EB483F, about 0.3% of slide width, running from about y 31.5% to y 78% at about x 52.9%, with round dots.

### Slide 59

**Text**

**Layout**

Centred title at top with a left-aligned subtitle beneath it, starting at the same left margin as the title block. Two equal circles sit side by side in the middle band of the slide, separated by a vertical dotted red line, with the footer line (presenter name, date, slide number) at the bottom right.

Two solid filled circles of equal size (each about 22.1% wide and 39.3% tall of the slide) and a vertical dotted line between them. Left circle: dark grey #535353 with the text 'Anything ‘boring’ will now be automated' centred in white. Right circle: red #EB483F with the text 'What industries need boring work? What can you split apart?' centred in white. Dotted line: vertical, red #EB483F, running from about y 33.9% to 72.9% at x about 59.1% of slide width, with small round dots, stroke about 0.3% of slide width. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 solid colour fill covering the whole slide.

### Slide 62

**Text**

Amazon, Google and Meta / don’t know what these are, / or why you buy them / Source: ChatGPT / Benedict Evans / –– May 2026 / 62

**Layout**

Two-column split: left light-grey panel holds the title block at about 25.6% to 45% of slide height, left-aligned at x 4.6%, with the source line anchored near the bottom-left at y 90.9%. Right half is a full-bleed photograph from x 48.7% to the right edge, full height. Footer row at y 91.1% spans the left panel: Benedict Evans bold at x 74.9% is actually on the photo side, with date at x 83.1% and page number 62 at x 94.3%, all sitting over the photograph.

**Visuals**

One photograph occupying the right side, x 48.7% to 100%, full height (1536 by 1024 pixels, cropped to fill). Subject: a large automated-style warehouse fulfilment floor with long rows of metal racking stacked with cardboard boxes, a conveyor line of grey roller belts running diagonally from the middle distance to the right foreground, and workers in yellow high-visibility vests standing at tables with monitors and parcels. In the foreground a woman in a yellow vest and jeans stands at a workbench with two monitors, cables, a stack of cardboard boxes and a rack of folders; yellow support pillars and overhead ceiling lights run across the upper area. Composition: deep one-point perspective receding to a vanishing point at the centre-left; camera elevated looking down the aisle. Lighting: bright overhead fluorescent light, neutral and cool. Dominant colours: tan cardboard brown, safety yellow, steel grey, white highlights. Full-bleed, not boxed, no overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background light neutral grey #F2F2F2 on left; right half sits on white #FFFFFF behind the photograph. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.56% of slide height per line with generous leading (about 6.6% line pitch). Footer and source in MessinaSans, Book weight, 1.85% of slide height; author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6. No shapes, lines, rules, shadows or gradients.

Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
64

**Layout**

Two-band layout on a light grey field. A grey header band occupies the top fifth of the slide with the title left-aligned at about 20.5% from the left edge and the subtitle beneath it on the same left edge. A white content band fills the middle, holding the chart title at top left, a vertical legend at the far left (x about 4.8%) and a five-bar 100% stacked column chart spanning x about 17% to 95%. A grey footer band holds the source line at the same left edge as the title and, at the right, the author name, date and page number 65 right-aligned to about 95%.

**Visuals**

Chart: 100% stacked column chart titled US consumer search preference (September 2025). Y axis 0% to 100% in 25% steps with dotted horizontal gridlines at 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%. X categories left to right: All, Under 30, 30-45, 45-60, 60-80, Older. Five stacked series bottom to top: Always GenAI (red), Mostly GenAI (pale pink), Mixed (mid grey), Mostly seach (dark grey), Always search (black). No values are printed on the bars; estimates follow. All: Always GenAI about 5%, Mostly GenAI about 11%, Mixed about 28%, Mostly seach about 26%, Always search about 30%. Under 30: Always GenAI about 8%, Mostly GenAI about 17%, Mixed about 33%, Mostly seach about 24%, Always search about 18%. 30-45: Always GenAI about 8%, Mostly GenAI about 16%, Mixed about 34%, Mostly seach about 24%, Always search about 18%. 45-60: Always GenAI about 4%, Mostly GenAI about 10%, Mixed about 31%, Mostly seach about 28%, Always search about 27%. 60-80: Always GenAI about 1.5%, Mostly GenAI about 4.5%, Mixed about 18%, Mostly seach about 28%, Always search about 48%. Older: Always GenAI about 0%, Mostly GenAI about 0%, Mixed about 14%, Mostly seach about 18%, Always search about 68%. Legend: vertical list at top left of chart with square swatches in the same five colours, top to bottom Always search, Mostly seach, Mixed, Mostly GenAI, Always GenAI. No highlighted points. Bars are flat with thin white separators between segments.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 across the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey header and footer bands (#F1F1F1) around a white content band (#FFFFFF). Title in a serif face resembling MessinaModern Regular, colour #545454, size about 5.6% of slide height, regular weight. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of height, colour #545454. Chart title and labels in a geometric sans resembling MessinaSans, Regular and SemiBold, about 1.85% to 2.2% of height, colour #535353 to #545554. Footer author name in bold sans #A6A6A6, date and page number in book-weight sans #A6A6A6. Chart bar colours: Always search #000000, Mostly seach #535353, Mixed #A7A7A7, Mostly GenAI about #F8DAD8, Always GenAI about #EB4A40. Gridlines are thin dotted #A7A7A7-ish lines. Bars separated by thin white strokes. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 66

**Text**

What are the new questions?
What can you ask an LLM that you could never ask deterministic software?
“Show me the
data Bob’s
talking about
on this call”
“What were the
top concerns on
customer calls
in the last six
months?”
What pricing
changes would
improve our
churn?
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
66

**Layout**

Three large circles sit in a single horizontal row across the centre-right of the slide, separated by two small right-pointing triangles, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top and the footer at lower right. The title starts at about x 20.5% and the subtitle at about x 20.6%, both on the same left edge; the circles span about x 19.5% to 96.2%, and the footer sits at y 91% with the page number at the right margin near x 95%.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles and two triangles, no photographs or charts. Circle 1 is dark grey #535353, about 22.1% wide and 39.3% tall, at x 19.5% y 33.9%, containing the quotation “Show me the data Bob’s talking about on this call”. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353, same size, at x 46.8% y 33.9%, containing “What were the top concerns on customer calls in the last six months?”. Circle 3 is red #EB483F, same size, at x 74.1% y 33.9%, containing “What pricing changes would improve our churn?”, and is the only coloured element, marking the final step. Two light grey right-pointing triangles (about #A6A6A6, roughly 4% wide and 7% tall) sit between the circles: the first at about x 43.2% y 49.8%, the second at about x 70.7% y 49.8%, each pointing right from the gap toward the next circle, indicating sequence. The triangles are not connected by lines. Each circle’s text is centred horizontally and vertically, set in four or five short lines. Nothing overlaps.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 fill covering the entire slide. Flat, minimal look with no gradients, shadows or corner radii; circles are perfectly round solid fills with no outline. Background light grey #F1F1F1. Title in a serif display face, MessinaModern-Regular (a modern serif, close to Century-style serif), regular weight, colour #545454 (dark grey), size about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif family, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Circle text in MessinaModern-Book, white #FFFFFF, about 3.3% of slide height, regular weight. Footer in MessinaSans, with ‘Benedict Evans’ in Bold and the date and page number in Book, colour #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height. Accent colours: dark grey #535353 for circles, red #EB483F for the final circle, light grey #A6A6A6 for arrows and footer.

### Slide 67

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A simple two-column comparison of task and job that uses space and short phrases to make a conceptual distinction easy to read.

**Text**

What’s the task and what’s the job?
What do you actually want? What are you trying to do? How do you split the music from the plastic?
Get the SKU,
write the code,
build the XLS,
write the spec
Task
What’s the hard
part?
Implicit
knowledge,
opinion, taste,
ideas…
Job
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
67

**Layout**

Two-line header block sits top-left with the title at x 20.5% and the subtitle beneath it at x 20.6%, both left-aligned. Three circles sit on a horizontal centre line at about y 34% to 73%, spaced across the middle of the slide, with the dark circle at left, a dotted outline circle in the centre and the red circle at right. The footer runs along the bottom right at y 91% with the author name, date and page number on one line, and there is no visible slide number outside that footer.

**Visuals**

Diagram of three circles in a row linked by two small grey triangular arrows. Left: solid dark grey (#535353) filled circle with white serif text 'Get the SKU, / write the code, / build the XLS, / write the spec' followed by 'Task'. Centre: dotted-outline circle with a dark grey dotted stroke (about 0.3% of slide height) and no fill, containing bold dark grey text 'What’s the hard part?'. Right: solid red (#EB483F) filled circle with white serif text 'Implicit / knowledge, / opinion, taste, / ideas…' followed by 'Job'. Between the left circle and the centre circle is a light grey (#A6A6A6) triangle pointing left; between the centre circle and the right circle is a light grey triangle pointing right. Both triangles are vertically centred on the circles' midline. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal look on a light warm grey ground. Title and subtitle in dark grey #545454 in Messina Modern Regular, a transitional serif; title about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Circle text is white Messina Modern Book about 3.3% of slide height, with the centre question in Messina Modern SemiBold #545454. Accents are the red #EB483F circle and dark grey #535353 circle; the dotted circle uses dark grey dots. Shapes are plain circles with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. Footer in Messina Sans, light grey #A6A6A6, with the name in Bold and the date in Book, about 1.9% of slide height.

The footer (name, date, page number) sits bottom-right at y≈91%, and the page number is right-aligned to about 95.5%. The left margin is empty.

Three circles and two triangles, no photographs or logos. Left: a solid dark grey (#535353) filled circle, about 22.1% wide by 39.3% tall, holding white serif text. Between the dotted circle and the right circle there is a right-pointing solid grey (#A6A6A6) triangle at about x 70.7%, y 49.8%, same size. None of the shapes overlap each other or the text.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal look on a light grey background. Circle text is MessinaModern-Book in white (#FFFFFF) at about 3.3% of slide height; the centre text is MessinaModern-SemiBold in #545454 at the same size. Below, a white band spans the slide width with six equal-width columns separated by thin light grey gutters; the first column at the left is blank, and the five labelled columns carry a curved arrow path rising left to right and a label at the bottom. The footer sits in a grey strip at the bottom right with author name, date and slide number on one line.

**Visuals**

A stepped sequence of five curved arrows, one per labelled column, each rising from lower left to higher right and ending in an arrowhead that points into the next column. Arrows 1 to 4 are solid grey (#808080) lines about 0.4% of slide height thick with grey filled triangular arrowheads. Arrow 5 (Generative AI) is a red (#EB483F) dotted line with a red filled triangular arrowhead, and it rises higher and ends near the top of the final column, implying a steeper trajectory. Labels beneath each arrow read Mainframes, PCs, Web, Smartphones and Generative AI, with the last label in red. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel covering the middle band from about 20.5% to 86% of slide height. Title and subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif, dark grey #545454; title is about 5.6% of slide height and subtitle about 2.6%. Column labels in MessinaModern-Book serif, #545454 except the red #EB483F Generative AI label, about 3.0% of slide height. Footer in MessinaSans, sans-serif: name bold, date and page number book weight, all #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height. Column dividers are thin light grey lines in the #F1F1F1 tone. Arrow lines are plain with no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 72

**Text**

How many times have we / been here before? / Source: IBM / Benedict Evans –– May 2026 / 72 / 150 Extra Engineers / An IBM Electronic Calculator speeds through thousands of intricate computations so quickly that on many complex problems it's just like having 150 EXTRA Engineers. / No longer must valuable engineering personnel . . . now in critical shortage . . . spend priceless creative time at routine repetitive figuring. / Thousands of IBM Electronic Business Machines . . . vital to our nation's defense . . . are at work for science, industry, and the armed forces, in laboratories, factories, and offices, helping to meet urgent demands for greater production. / IBM / INTERNATIONAL BUSINESS MACHINES

**Layout**

Two equal columns split at the vertical centre: a light grey left half holds the title block at the left margin (about 4.6% from the left edge) and the source line at bottom left; a white right half holds a single framed vintage advertisement image centred in that half, with a footer row along the bottom (author, date, page number) aligned to the image's right edge area. The title sits in the upper-middle of the left column, left-aligned; there is no header and no slide-level logo.

**Visuals**

One photograph of a vintage print advertisement, boxed with a soft drop shadow, not cropped, not full-bleed, no overlay or tint. Subject: an illustrated 1950s-style advertisement titled '150 Extra Engineers'. A man with short brown wavy hair, white collared shirt and teal tie holds a large slide rule diagonally across his body with both hands and looks toward the viewer. Behind him, rows of disembodied male heads each hold a slide rule, filling the upper half of the frame. In the lower left, a man in a brown suit stands beside a grey IBM electronic calculator cabinet, reaching to its console. Lower right holds the body copy in small serif black type, the heading '150 Extra Engineers' in bold brown serif with 'Extra' underlined, and the IBM logo: a bold 'IBM' in a yellow-orange outlined box overlapping an orange atom diagram, followed by 'INTERNATIONAL BUSINESS MACHINES' in small caps. Composition: portrait framing, subject centred-left, head near top third. Lighting: flat, even illustration lighting. Dominant colours: cream white #F7F3E8 background, tan skin tones #C8875A, teal tie #2E6F7A, grey machine #8A8F94, brown suit #5A3A24.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left half solid #F2F2F2 light grey, right half solid #FFFFFF white. Left half background #F2F2F2 light grey, right half background #FFFFFF white, with no gradients. Title text colour #545454 dark grey, body text colour #545454, footer author #A6A6A6 grey, date #A6A6A6, page number #A6A6A6. Title typeface MessinaModern-Regular, a serif (transitional/modern), regular weight, roughly 5.6% of slide height per line. Source and footer typeface MessinaSans-Book, a sans-serif, regular weight, roughly 1.9% of slide height; author name in MessinaSans-Bold. Image has a soft grey drop shadow beneath and no border radius.

### Slide 73

**Text**

All AI questions have one of two answers
History doesn’t repeat, but it generally rhymes
“No-one knows”
Insert your question here…
“What happened the last time that everything changed?”
Benedict Evans –– May 2026 73

**Layout**

Single-column composition with a left-aligned title block at the top, starting at about 20.5% from the left edge, and a row of three equal circles centred vertically in the lower-middle area. The title sits at about 5.6% from the top with a subtitle directly beneath it. A footer line sits at the bottom right, with the author name, date and slide number on one baseline at about 91% from the top. The left side below the title is empty, and the right margin ends at about 94.4%.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles in a horizontal row, each 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height, with identical size and vertical alignment. The left and right circles are dark grey #535353 and carry white quotations. The centre circle is red #EB483F and carries white text. Between adjacent circles are two small grey triangles #A6A6A6 with no outline: the left triangle, between the left and centre circles, points left, and the right triangle, between the centre and right circles, points right. Both triangles sit at the vertical middle of the circles. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Flat, minimal look on a light grey background. Title is set in MessinaModern-Regular serif, dark grey #545454, about 5.56% of slide height. Subtitle is MessinaModern-Regular, #545454, about 2.59% of slide height. Circle text is MessinaModern-Book serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.33% of slide height. Footer is MessinaSans, about 1.85% of slide height, light grey #A6A6A6; author name is bold and the rest is book weight. Shapes are plain with no outlines, no shadows, no gradients and no rounded corners beyond the circles themselves. Accent colour is the red #EB483F on the centre circle.

### Slide 74

**Text**

“No-one knows anything” - William Goldman
For every new platform, we forget how many ideas failed and how unclear everything was
Internet
AOL, Yahoo,
Pointcast, Flash,
VRML, portals…
Sun? Netscape?
Mobile internet
i-mode, J2ME, WAP,
JOYN, DVB-H,
decks, preloads…
Nokia? RIM?
Generative AI?
Browsers? MCP?
Voice? App stores?
Wearables? GEO?
Benedict Evans
–– May 2026
74

**Layout**

Three white circles of equal size sit in one horizontal row across the middle of the slide, with small grey triangular arrows between them, pointing right. The title sits top-left aligned at about x 20.5%, with a one-line subtitle beneath it at the same left edge. The footer line (author, date, slide number) sits bottom right at about y 91%, with the slide number at the right edge near x 94.4%. No visible grid or column rules; the left margin of about 20% is empty.

**Visuals**

Three identical-size circles arranged left to right, each containing a bold heading and centred lines of text. Circle 1 (Internet) and circle 2 (Mobile internet) have dark grey outlines (#545454, about 0.3% of slide height stroke) on white fill. Circle 3 (Generative AI?) has the same white fill but a red outline (#E84B3C, same stroke weight), marking it as the highlighted item. Between circles 1 and 2 and between circles 2 and 3 sit two solid grey right-pointing triangles (#A6A6A6), each about 1.9% wide and 7% tall, vertically centred on the circles' midline. No chart, photo, or logo. No estimates needed.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Flat light-grey background #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle in dark grey #545454, set in a serif face closely resembling Messina Modern (Regular weight), title large, subtitle small. Circle headings in #545454 SemiBold serif. Body lines in #545454 Regular serif. Accent red #E84B3C used only for the third circle outline. Arrow triangles #A6A6A6. Footer in light grey #A6A6A6, sans-serif (Messina Sans style): author name Bold, date and slide number Book weight. Title about 5.6% of slide height, body about 3% of slide height, footer about 1.9%. Circle outlines are plain strokes with no shadows and no corner radii, since the shapes are circles. Triangles are flat with no outline.

### Slide 75

**Text**

What will change? “It depends”
Sometimes software eats the world, and sometimes it only nibbles
New York taxis versus Uber (trips per day)
US hotels versus Airbnb (room revenue)
Uber etc
Taxis
Hotels
Airbnb
0
2015 / 2020 / 2024
0
2015 / 2020 / 2024
Source: TLC, Airbnb (NA GBV), Morgan Stanley
Benedict Evans –– May 2026 75

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band (top about 20% of slide) holding a serif title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20.5%, over a white content area (y 20.5% to 86%) with two side-by-side line charts, and a light grey footer band (y 86% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Left margin about 20.5%; right edge of content about 95%. Title sits top-left inside header; slide number bottom-right in footer.

**Visuals**

Two line charts, both with smoothed curves, no legend, series labelled directly at line ends. Left chart: 'New York taxis versus Uber (trips per day)'. X axis years 2015 to 2024 with labels 2015, 2020, 2024 printed; y axis starts at 0 (baseline labelled 0), no units printed; faint dotted horizontal gridlines at five levels. Series 1 'Uber etc' in red (#E8443A approx.) rises from about 15% of plot height at 2015 to a peak near 2018.8 (about 90% of plot height), drops to a trough at 2020 (about 45%), then rises to about 88% by 2024. Series 2 'Taxis' in dark grey (#4D4D4D approx.) falls from about 65% at 2015 to about 25% at 2020 trough (about 10% plot height), then flat near 12% through 2024. The lines cross near 2016.5. Right chart: 'US hotels versus Airbnb (room revenue)'. X axis 2015, 2020, 2024; y baseline 0; dotted gridlines at five levels. Series 'Hotels' dark grey rises from about 60% of plot height at 2015 to about 70% at 2019, dips to about 40% at 2020, then climbs to about 90% by 2024. Series 'Airbnb' red starts about 2017 near 2% of plot height and rises gently to about 12% by 2024. Values not printed; all positions are estimates.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide fill with a white #FFFFFF band across the middle. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF central band. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, dark grey #545454, sized about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in same serif, #545454, about 2.6%. Chart headings in MessinaSans-SemiBold sans-serif, #545554, about 2.2%. Chart labels and axis numbers in MessinaSans-Regular, #535353, about 1.85%. Accent colour red about #E8443A for Uber and Airbnb series; dark grey #4D4D4D for taxis and hotels. Thin dotted grey gridlines and a thin solid grey baseline. Line strokes about 2px, no shadows, no rounded shapes.

**Layout**

Single chart slide on a two-band frame. A full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about x 20.5%, and a full-width light grey footer band holds the source line, the author line and the page number. The white middle band (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holds a chart with its title at x 20.6%, a legend at the far left (x 5%), and a bar chart spanning roughly x 19% to 95% and y 35% to 83%. The footer source lines start at x 20.5% and the author line sits at about x 75% to 89%, with the page number at x 94.4%, at the bottom right.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked vertical bar chart, one bar per year from 2000 to 2025 (26 bars). Title: 'Global recorded music revenue, trade value ($bn, 2025 dollars)'. Y axis: 0 to 50 in $bn (2025 dollars), labelled 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each 10 step. X axis: years 2000 to 2025, labelled at 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020, 2025, with a thin grey baseline. Series, bottom to top: Physical (dark grey #535353), Streaming (red #E84B3E approx.), Downloads (pale pink #F7D9D6 approx.), Usage fees* (mid grey #A6A6A6 approx.). Values are not printed; estimates in $bn: Physical about 39 (2000), 39.7 (2001), 36 (2002), 32.5 (2003), 30.6 (2004), 27.7 (2005), 24.4 (2006), 20.2 (2007), 16.8 (2008), 14.4 (2009), 12.2 (2010), 10.6 (2011), 9.3 (2012), 8.3 (2013), 7.4 (2014), 7.1 (2015), 6.5 (2016), 6.0 (2017), 5.3 (2018), 4.9 (2019), 4.7 (2020), 5.0 (2021), 4.9 (2022), 5.4 (2023), 5.1 (2024), 5.4 (2025). Streaming first appears around 2008 and is about 27 by 2025 (total stack height about 27.5 in 2025 with streaming from about 5.4 to about 27.4). Totals per bar about: 39 (2000), 39.7 (2001), 37 (2002), 33.8 (2003), 32.3 (2004), 30.8 (2005), 28.8 (2006), 25.8 (2007), 23.8 (2008), 21.8 (2009), 19.8 (2010), 19.6 (2011), 19 (2012), 18.4 (2013), 17.8 (2014), 18.5 (2015), 19.3 (2016), 20.7 (2017), 22.1 (2018), 23.8 (2019), 25.4 (2020), 27.7 (2021), 28.4 (2022), 29.8 (2023), 30.5 (2024), 31.6 (2025). Legend: top-left, stacked vertically in order Usage fees*, Downloads, Streaming, Physical, each with a small square swatch (about 2% by 2% of the slide) and label in MessinaSans Regular. No highlighted points. Bars are flat-topped with thin white separators between segments.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide background with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Clean, minimal editorial look. Background is light grey #F1F1F1 at top and bottom with a white #FFFFFF middle band. Title in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, roman, about 5.6% of slide height, colour #545454). Subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title and axis labels in a grotesque sans (MessinaSans SemiBold for chart title, Regular for labels) at about 1.85% of slide height, colours #535554 and #545454. Footer text in MessinaSans Regular about 1.78%, colour #545454; author name in MessinaSans Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in MessinaSans Book #A6A6A6. Accent colour is red #E84B3E for streaming. Gridlines are thin dotted grey. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.
